is not congruential, because its provability does not entail equality of the semantical values, only of their first coordinates. It is therefore useful to define the strong counterparts of the two connectives. In particular,
the strong equivalence
\alpha\Leftrightarrow\beta\coloneqq(\alpha\leftrightarrowtriangle\beta)\wedge(\neg\alpha\leftrightarrowtriangle\neg\beta)
is congruential.

Observe that, thanks to the peculiar nature of the logic, some of the usual definitions of Łukasiewicz connectives only work as weak equivalences when lifted to
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
: namely,
\alpha\wedge\beta\leftrightarrowtriangle(\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\beta)\&\alpha
and
\alpha\vee\beta\leftrightarrowtriangle((\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\beta)\rightarrowtriangle\beta)
are valid, but it is no longer the case when both sides of the equivalences are
\neg
-negated.

Definition 4.18
.

The logic
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
is axiomatised by the following axioms and rules:

(w)

\alpha\rightarrowtriangle(\beta\rightarrowtriangle\alpha)

(
\vee
)

((\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\beta)\rightarrowtriangle\beta)\leftrightarrowtriangle\alpha\vee\beta

(sf)

(\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\beta)\rightarrowtriangle((\beta\rightarrowtriangle\gamma)\rightarrowtriangle(\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\gamma))

(
\&
)

\alpha\&\beta\Leftrightarrow{\sim}(\alpha\rightarrowtriangle{\sim}\beta)

(waj)

((\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\beta)\rightarrowtriangle\beta)\rightarrowtriangle((\beta\rightarrowtriangle\alpha)\rightarrowtriangle\alpha)

(
\wedge
)

\alpha\wedge\beta\leftrightarrowtriangle(\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\beta)\&\alpha

(co)

({\sim}\beta\rightarrowtriangle{\sim}\alpha)\rightarrowtriangle(\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\beta)

(
\neg\wedge
)

\neg(\alpha\wedge\beta)\Leftrightarrow\neg\alpha\vee\neg\beta

(dn
\neg
)

\neg\neg\alpha\Leftrightarrow\alpha

(
\neg\vee
)

\neg(\alpha\vee\beta)\Leftrightarrow\neg\alpha\wedge\neg\beta

(
\neg{\rightarrowtriangle}
)

\neg(\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\beta)\leftrightarrowtriangle(\alpha\&\neg\beta)

(
\neg{\sim}
)

\neg{\sim}\alpha\leftrightarrowtriangle\alpha

(MP)

\alpha,\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\beta\vdash\beta

The axioms (w, sf, waj, co,
\vee
,
\wedge
) with the rule (MP) axiomatise Łukasiewicz logic in terms of weak implication, (
\&
) is the (strong) definition of the fusion. The remaining are
\neg
-axioms, analogous to those of the Nelson’s paraconsistent logic
\mathsf{N}4
.

We can, up to weak equivalence, consider formulas in the
\neg
-normal form:

Lemma 4.19

(NNF)
.

For each formula
\alpha
in
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
there is a formula in
\neg
-negation normal form
\alpha^{\star}
such that

\vdash_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\alpha\leftrightarrowtriangle\alpha^{\star}.

As (MP) is the only rule, the logic possesses a local deduction theorem (LDT) in terms of the weak implication, as in
Ł
(cf.
[
10
, Ch.I,Th. 1.2.10]
).

We proceed to prove the finite strong standard completeness (FSSC) of the logic
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
. We do so by means of a reduction to Łukasiewicz logic, which itself is well-known to be finite strong standard complete, i.e. finite strong complete w.r.t. the standard MV algebra
[0,1]_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}

[
28
, Th. 3.2.14]
.

Lemma 4.20

(Reduction of
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
to
Ł
)
.

W.l.o.g. we assume that
\Gamma,\alpha
consist of formulas in
\neg
-
\mathsf{NNF}
.

\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\alpha
iff
\Gamma,\Sigma\vdash_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\alpha
for
\Sigma
a finite set consisting of instances of
\neg
-axioms.

Proof.

This is almost immediate:
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
is an extension of
Ł
, so any proof in
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
can be understood as a proof in
Ł
, with additional assumptions consisting of all the (finitely many) instances of
\neg
-axioms occurring in the proof.
Observe, that we can equivalently include (finitely many) additional instances of
\neg
-axioms for subformulas of
\Gamma,\alpha
in
\Sigma
, and the claim remains true.
∎

Lemma 4.21

(FSSC)
.

for
\Gamma,\alpha
a finite set of formulas in
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
,

\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\alpha\ \ \mbox{iff}\ \ \Gamma\vDash_{[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\alpha.

Proof.

W.l.o.g. we assume that
\Gamma,\alpha
consist of formulas in
\neg
-negation normal form. Assume that
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\alpha
. By the Lemma
4.20
, it is the case if and only if
\Gamma,\Sigma\vdash_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\alpha
for
\Sigma
a finite set consisting of instances of
\neg
-axioms. We will add the following (finitely many) formulas to
\Sigma
: (i) instances of
\neg
-axioms for subformulas of
\Gamma,\alpha
, (ii) for each subformula
\beta
of
\Gamma,\alpha
of the form listed on the left, we include an equivalence listed on the right (
\beta^{\neg}
denotes the NNF of
\neg\beta
, i.e.
(\neg\beta)^{\star}
):

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle\wedge\beta_{2}

\displaystyle((\beta_{1})^{\neg}\vee(\beta_{2})^{\neg})

\displaystyle\leftrightarrowtriangle(\beta_{1}\wedge\beta_{2})^{\neg}

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle\vee\beta_{2}

\displaystyle((\beta_{1})^{\neg}\wedge(\beta_{2})^{\neg})

\displaystyle\leftrightarrowtriangle(\beta_{1}\vee\beta_{2})^{\neg}

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle\rightarrowtriangle\beta_{2}

\displaystyle(\beta_{1}\&(\beta_{2})^{\neg})

\displaystyle\leftrightarrowtriangle(\beta_{1}\rightarrowtriangle\beta_{2})^{\neg}

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle\&\beta_{2}

\displaystyle(\beta_{1}\rightarrowtriangle{\sim}\beta_{2})

\displaystyle\leftrightarrowtriangle(\beta_{1}\&\beta_{2})^{\neg}

\displaystyle{\sim}\beta

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle\leftrightarrowtriangle({\sim}\beta)^{\neg}

We call the resulting finite set
\Sigma^{\prime}
. First we observe that all the equivalences listed are provable from
\Sigma
by means of the logic
Ł
and
\neg
-axioms instances for subformulas of
\Gamma,\alpha
(we opt for enlisting them explicitly for convenience). In particular, we have:

\Gamma,\Sigma\vdash_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\alpha\ \ \mbox{iff}\ \ \Gamma,\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\alpha.

Assume now that
\Gamma,\Sigma^{\prime}\nvdash_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\alpha
. By the FSSC of
Ł
, we know there is an evaluation
v:\Phi\to[0,1]
, where
\Phi
consists of (i) literals from
\Gamma,\Sigma^{\prime},\alpha
, and (ii)
\neg
-subformulas of
\Sigma^{\prime}
, so that:

v(\Gamma\cup\Sigma^{\prime})\subseteq\{1\}
while
v(\alpha)<1
.
We define an evaluation
v^{\prime}:\mathtt{Prop}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}\to[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
by

v^{\prime}(p)\coloneqq(v(p),v(\neg p)).

Next, we can easily prove by induction, for each subformula
\beta
of
\Gamma,\alpha
, that the extended valuation satisfies

v^{\prime}(\beta)\coloneqq(v(\beta),v(\beta^{\neg})).

The proof essentially uses the fact that formulas contained in
\Sigma^{\prime}
are
v
-evaluated by
1
.

To conclude, we observe that because
v(\alpha)<1
, by the above we have
v^{\prime}_{1}(\alpha)<1
. Similarly, because for each
\gamma\in\Gamma

v(\gamma)=1
, we have that
v^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma)=1
, making all formulas from
\Gamma
designated and
\alpha
not designated, as desired.
∎

Example 4.2

(What we can express in
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
)
.

Recall first that for each
v
,
v({\sim}p)
is always on the vertical, classical line. Observe that, in contrast with
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
, we can now directly express incompleteness and contradictoriness: indeed,
v({\sim}p\rightarrowtriangle\neg p)
is designated if and only if
v(p)
lays within the right triangle,
v(\neg p\rightarrowtriangle{\sim}p)
is designated if and only if
v(p)
lays within the left triangle, and
v({\sim}p\leftrightarrowtriangle\neg p)
is designated if and only if
v(p)
lays on the vertical line.
In
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
, we can thus define ‘
p
is not incomplete’ as
{\sim}p\rightarrowtriangle\neg p
, and ‘
p
is not contradictory’ as
\neg p\rightarrowtriangle{\sim}p
, and ’
p
is classical’ as
{\sim}p\leftrightarrowtriangle\neg p
.

Moreover,
v((p\rightarrowtriangle q)\wedge(\neg p\rightarrowtriangle\neg q))
is designated if and only if both
v_{1}(p)\leq v_{1}(q)
and
v_{2}(p)\leq v_{2}(q)
, and therefore the formula captures the
information order
(the left-right horizontal order) on the
[0,1]^{\Join}
.

4.3
Two-layered logic for probabilities

In this subsection, we introduce a two-layered logic
\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
designed to capture reasoning about non-standard probabilities of Subsection
3.1
. The
inner layer
consists of the language, semantics and axiomatisation of the logic
\mathsf{BD}
as described in Subsection
2.2
. The
outer layer
consists of the language, semantics and axiomatisation of the logic
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
as described in Subsection
4.2.1
. The two layers are connected with a single modality
\mathsf{Pr}
(which can be read as ‘probably’). An application of the modality to an inner formula yields an atomic modal outer formula, which is to be interpreted using a probability function over the underlying probabilistic
\mathsf{BD}
model, outputting a value in the outer algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
. Other applications of the modality are forbidden in the syntax, in particular, modalities do not nest.

Definition 4.22

(Language and semantics of
\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
)
.

The two-layered language of the logic
\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
is of the form
(\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}},\{\mathsf{Pr}\},\mathscr{L}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}})
, with
\mathsf{Pr}
being a unary modality, and it is defined as follows:

inner formulas:

formulas of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}

atomic modal formulas:

Let
\phi\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
, then
\mathsf{Pr}\phi
is a modal atomic formula.

outer formulas:

Let
\mathsf{Pr}\phi
range over modal atomic formulas, the set of outer formulas is defined via the following grammar, with the connectives of
\mathscr{L}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}
:

\alpha\coloneqq\mathsf{Pr}\phi\mid\neg\alpha\mid\alpha\rightarrow\alpha\mid{\sim}\alpha\mid\triangle\alpha.

Other connectives —
\bot,\wedge,\vee,\&
,
\ominus
and
\oplus
— can be defined as in
\mathscr{L}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}
of Definition
4.11
.

The semantics of
\mathscr{L}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}
is given over a
\mathsf{BD}
probabilistic model
\mathscr{M}=(W,v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{p})
(cf. Section
3.1
), and the outer algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
, as follows:

inner formulas:

for each
\phi\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
the model provides sets
|\phi|^{+}=\{w\mid w\vDash^{+}\phi\}
and
|\phi|^{-}=\{w\mid w\vDash^{-}\phi\}
, as described in Definition
2.2
.

atomic modal formulas:

for each atomic modal formula,
v^{\mathscr{M}}(\mathsf{Pr}\phi)
in
[0,1]^{\Join}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
is defined as

v^{\mathscr{M}}(\mathsf{Pr}\phi)=(v^{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\mathsf{Pr}\phi),v^{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(\mathsf{Pr}\phi))\coloneqq(\mathtt{p}(|\phi|^{+}),\mathtt{p}(|\phi|^{-}))=\left(\sum\limits_{w\vDash^{+}\phi}\mathtt{m}_{\mathtt{p}}(w),\sum\limits_{w\vDash^{-}\phi}\mathtt{m}_{\mathtt{p}}(w)\right).

outer formulas:

The map
v^{\mathscr{M}}
is extended to the outer formulas in an expected manner, following the Definition
4.11
of the interpretation of
\mathscr{L}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}
connectives in the algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
.

The outer formula
\alpha
is said to be valid in
\mathscr{M}
, if
v^{\mathscr{M}}(\alpha)
is designated (i.e.
v^{\mathscr{M}}(\alpha)=(1,0)
). It is said to be valid, if it is valid in all
\mathsf{BD}
probabilistic models.
The consequence relation for
\Gamma,\alpha
a set of outer formulas is defined as

\Gamma\vDash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha\ \ \mbox{iff}\ \ \forall\mathscr{M}(v^{\mathscr{M}}[\Gamma]\subseteq(1,0)\Rightarrow v^{\mathscr{M}}(\alpha)=(1,0)).

Definition 4.23

(Axiomatisation of
\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
)
.

The two-layered axiomatisation of logic
\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}=(\mathsf{BD},M_{p},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2})
consists of the following parts:

inner logic:

the axiomatisation of
\mathsf{BD}
in the language
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
of Definition
2.1

modal axioms:

M_{p}
, where
\varphi,\psi\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
:

\displaystyle\{\mathsf{Pr}\varphi

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathsf{Pr}\psi\mid\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{Pr}\neg\varphi

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\neg\mathsf{Pr}\varphi

\displaystyle\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\vee\psi)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\ominus\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\psi))\oplus\mathsf{Pr}\psi

outer logic:

the axiomatisation of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
of Definition
4.12
, instantiated in outer formulas.

A
proof
of a formula
\alpha
from a set of assumptions
\Gamma
in the logic
\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
(in short
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
), where
\Gamma\cup\{\alpha\}
is a finite set of
outer formulas
, is a finite tree which can be parsed as follows: the smallest proofs consist of either (i) a finite tree labelled by
\mathsf{BD}
formulas, proving
\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi
, followed by the instance
\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{Pr}\psi
of the first modal axiom, or (ii) a node labelled by an instance of either of the remaining two modal axioms or an instance of an axiom of the outer logic in the outer language, or (iii) an assumption
\beta\in\Gamma
in the outer language. More complex proofs are then formed inductively in the standard manner, by means of syntactically correct applications of the rules of the outer logic to smaller proofs. The root of the tree is to be labelled by
\alpha
.

Theorem 4.24

(FSSC of
\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
)
.

For each
\Gamma,\alpha
a finite set of outer formulas,

\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha\ \ \mbox{iff}\ \ \Gamma\vDash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha.

Proof.

The soundness and completeness proof essentially relies on the two-layered nature of the logic. We will use the following facts: (i) the inner logic
\mathsf{BD}
is strongly sound and complete w.r.t. its double-valuation semantics, and it is locally finite, (ii) the outer logic
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
is finitely strongly sound and complete w.r.t. the outer algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
, and (iii) the modal axioms are a sound and complete axiomatisation of probablities over
\mathsf{BD}
probabilistic models.

Soundness
: Given a proof
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
and any
\mathsf{BD}
probabilistic model
\mathscr{M}=(S,v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{p})
, we reason inductively: (i) we know that any proof of
\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi
is sound, therefore it entails that
|\varphi|^{+}\subseteq|\psi|^{+}
and
|\psi|^{-}\subseteq|\varphi|^{-}
in any
\mathsf{BD}
model, and thus in
\mathscr{M}
. This entails that
\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{+})\leq\mathtt{p}(|\psi|^{+})
and
\mathtt{p}(|\psi|^{-})\leq\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{-})
, and the following instance
\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{Pr}\psi
of the first modal axiom is justified, as it results in the value
(1,0)
in the outer algebra. The rest of the proof follows from the soundness of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
, provided the rest of the modal axioms are sound. Assume an instance
\mathsf{Pr}\neg\varphi\leftrightarrow\neg\mathsf{Pr}\varphi
of the second modal axiom: its left-hand side
\mathsf{Pr}\neg\varphi
amounts to
(\mathtt{p}(|\neg\varphi|^{+}),\mathtt{p}(|\neg\varphi|^{-}))=(\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{-}),\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{+}))
which is the semantics of
\neg\mathsf{Pr}\varphi
as desired.

Assume now an instance

\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\vee\psi)\leftrightarrow(\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\ominus\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\psi))\oplus\mathsf{Pr}\psi
of the third modal axiom: Its left-hand side
\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\vee\psi)
amounts to

(\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{+}\cup|\psi|^{+}),\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{-}\cap|\psi|^{-})).

The first coordinate we know to satisfy

\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{+}\cup|\psi|^{+})=\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{+})+\mathtt{p}(|\psi|^{+})-\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{+}\cap|\psi|^{+})

directly by the axiom (iii) of Definition
3.2
, while the second coordinate satisfies

\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{-}\cap|\psi|^{-})=\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{-})+\mathtt{p}(|\psi|^{-})-\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{-}\cup|\psi|^{-}).

The first coordinate immediately matches

v_{1}((\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\ominus\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\psi))\oplus\mathsf{Pr}\psi)=(v_{1}(\mathsf{Pr}\varphi)-v_{1}(\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\psi)))+v_{1}(\mathsf{Pr}\psi),

because
(|\varphi|^{+}\cap|\psi|^{+})\leq\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{+})
by monotonicity and therefore the truncated
\ominus_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
semantics outputs
v_{1}(\mathsf{Pr}\varphi)-v_{1}(\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\psi))
and does not trivialise to
0
. The second coordinate

v_{2}((\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\ominus\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\psi))\oplus\mathsf{Pr}\psi)=(v_{2}(\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\psi))\rightarrow_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}v_{2}(\mathsf{Pr}\varphi))\&_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}v_{2}(\mathsf{Pr}\psi)

and because now by monotonicity
\mathtt{p}(|\varphi\wedge\psi|^{-})=\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{-}\cup|\psi|^{-})\geq\mathtt{p}(|\varphi|^{-})
, the implication does not trivialise to
1
and this computes exactly as required.

Completeness
: Assume that
\Gamma\nvdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
. We will show that there is
\mathsf{BD}
probabilistic model validating
\Gamma
while refuting
\alpha
. We will use the canonical model
\mathscr{M}_{c}=(\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{Lit}),v_{c}^{+},v_{c}^{-})
of Definition
3.5
to do so.
Consider the finite set of
\mathsf{BD}
subformulas of modal atoms of
\Gamma,\Phi
to be given by

\mathtt{Sf}_{\Gamma,\alpha}\coloneqq\{\varphi\mid\exists\varphi^{\prime}:\mathsf{Pr}\varphi^{\prime}\mbox{ a modal atom of }\Gamma\cup\{\alpha\}\mbox{ and }\varphi\mbox{ a subformula of }\varphi^{\prime}\}.

Because
\mathsf{BD}
is locally finite, also the set

\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}\coloneqq\{\psi\mid\exists\varphi\in\mathtt{Sf}_{\Gamma,\alpha}\ \mbox{ and }\ \varphi\dashv\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi\}

is finite. Now define the finite set
\Sigma
of outer formulas to contain all instances of the modal axioms for formulas from
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
.

Given that
\Gamma\nvdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
, it is obviously also the case that
\Gamma,\Sigma\nvdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
.
Then we know, by FSSC of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
, that there is a refuting valuation

v:\{\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\mid\varphi\in\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}\}\to[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}},

i.e.
v[\Gamma\cup\Sigma]\subseteq\{(1,0)\}
while
v(\alpha)<(1,0)
. Because
v[\Sigma]\subseteq\{(1,0)\}
, and
\Sigma
in fact fully describes behaviour of a probability on the
\mathsf{BD}
formula algebra generated by propositional atoms from the set
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
, we define the probability of a
\mathsf{BD}
formula
\phi\in\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
to be

(\mathtt{p}^{+}(\varphi),\mathtt{p}^{-}(\varphi))=v(\mathsf{Pr}\varphi).

It is then a routine to check that this assignment indeed is a probability on the respective formula algebra. Therefore, by Theorem
3.4
, we know that this map can be extended to act as a probability on the powerset of the canonical
\mathsf{BD}
model

(\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{Lit}),v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{p}^{\prime})
with
\mathsf{Lit}
being the finite set of literals from the set
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
, i.e.
\mathtt{p}^{+}(\varphi)=\mathtt{p}^{\prime}(|\varphi|^{+})
and
\mathtt{p}^{-}(\varphi)=\mathtt{p}^{\prime}(|\varphi|^{-})
for each formula from the set
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Example 4.3
.

Assume for example that
{\sim}\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)
, i.e. that the positive probability of the contradiction is
0
. Observe that then positive probability of
(\varphi\vee\neg\varphi)
is
1
:

{\sim}\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}{\sim}\neg{\sim}\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}{\sim}{\sim}\neg\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\neg\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\vee\neg\varphi).

From the third axiom we then obtain

\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}(\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\ominus\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi))\oplus\mathsf{Pr}\neg\varphi

which is equivalent (and therefore provably so) to

\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}(\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi))\rightarrow\mathsf{Pr}\neg\varphi.

It is easy to prove that

{\sim}\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}(\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi))\leftrightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{Pr}\varphi
, and from that we obtain

{\sim}\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}{\sim}\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\rightarrow\neg\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}{\sim}{\neg}\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{Pr}\varphi.

As
{\sim}{\neg}\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{Pr}\varphi
and
\mathsf{Pr}\varphi\rightarrow{\sim}{\neg}\mathsf{Pr}\varphi
are provable from each other (cf. Example
4.1
), we can see that assuming that
{\sim}\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)
entails that
\mathsf{Pr}\varphi
is classical. On the other hand, assuming
\mathsf{Pr}\varphi
is classical, we can prove that

{\sim}\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)\leftrightarrow\mathsf{Pr}(\varphi\vee\neg\varphi)
.

4.4
Two-layered logics for belief and plausibility functions

In this subsection, we introduce two logics formalising two different two-dimensional interpretations of belief and plausibility. The first option is to treat the belief modality
\mathsf{B}
as a ‘Belnapian belief’ described in Section
3.3.1
. This way,
the negative support
of
\mathsf{B}\phi
is equal to
\mathtt{bel}(|\phi|^{-})
(i.e., the belief in
\phi
’s negative extension). The
plausibility of
\phi
can be defined in terms of its belief (cf. Remark
4.7
for the details).

The second option is to interpret belief and plausibility as two independent measures. In this approach,
\mathsf{B}\phi
’s negative support to be equal to the
plausibility of
\neg\phi
as presented in Section
3.3.2
. Dually, the negative support of
\mathsf{Pl}\phi

is the belief in
\neg\phi
.

These two approaches require two different logics.
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
is more suitable to formalise the Belnapian belief since the validity takes into account both positive and negative supports and since the belief modality should act in a symmetric manner. On the other hand,
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
is better suited to formalise the reasoning with independent
\mathtt{bel}
and
\mathtt{pl}
since it can separate positive and negative supports of a given statement (recall Example
4.2
).

4.4.1
Two-layered logic for belief functions

Definition 4.25

(Language and semantics of
\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
)
.

The two-layered language of the logic
\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
is of the form
(\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}},\{\mathsf{B}\},\mathscr{L}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}})
, with a unary modality
\mathsf{B}
, and is defined as follows:

inner formulas:

formulas of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}

atomic modal formulas:

Let
\phi\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
, then
\mathsf{B}\phi
is a modal atomic formula.

outer formulas:

Let
\mathsf{B}\phi
range over modal atomic formulas, the set of outer formulas is defined via the following grammar, with the connectives of
\mathscr{L}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}
:

\alpha\coloneqq\mathsf{B}\phi\mid\neg\alpha\mid\alpha\rightarrow\alpha\mid{\sim}\alpha\mid\triangle\alpha.

Other connectives:
\bot
,
\wedge
,
\vee
,
\&
,
\ominus
, and
\oplus
, can be defined as in
\mathscr{L}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}
of Definition
4.11
.

The semantics of the two-layered language is given over a
\mathsf{DS}
model
\mathscr{M}=(W,v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{bel})
, and the outer algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
, as follows:

inner formulas:

for each
\phi\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
the model provides sets
|\phi|^{+}=\{w\mid w\vDash^{+}\phi\}
and
|\phi|^{-}=\{w\mid w\vDash^{-}\phi\}
, as described in Definition
2.2
.

atomic modal formulas:

for each atomic modal formula
\mathsf{B}\phi
,
v^{\mathscr{M}}(\mathsf{B}\phi)
in
[0,1]^{\Join}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
is defined as

v^{\mathscr{M}}(\mathsf{B}\phi)=(v^{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\mathsf{B}\phi),v^{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(\mathsf{B}\phi))\coloneqq(\mathtt{bel}(|\phi|^{+}),\mathtt{bel}(|\phi|^{-}))=\left(\sum\limits_{X\subseteq|\phi|^{+}}\!\!\!\!\!\mathtt{m}_{\mathtt{bel}}(X),\sum\limits_{X\subseteq|\phi|^{-}}\!\!\!\!\!\mathtt{m}_{\mathtt{bel}}(X)\right).

outer formulas:

The map
v^{\mathscr{M}}
is extended to the outer formulas in an expected manner, following the Definition
4.11
of the interpretation of
\mathscr{L}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}
connectives in the algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
.

The outer formula
\alpha
is said to be valid in
\mathscr{M}
, if
v^{\mathscr{M}}(\alpha)
is designated (i.e.
v^{\mathscr{M}}(\alpha)=(1,0)
). It is said to be valid if it is valid in all
\mathsf{DS}
models.
The consequence relation for
\Gamma,\alpha
a set of outer formulas is defined as

\Gamma\vDash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha\ \ \mbox{iff}\ \ \forall\mathscr{M}(v^{\mathscr{M}}[\Gamma]\subseteq(1,0)\Rightarrow v^{\mathscr{M}}(\alpha)=(1,0)).

Remark 4.7
.

Note that we can also introduce a definable modality
\mathsf{Pl}
into
\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
. Indeed, since we can interpret
1-\mathtt{bel}(|\neg\phi|^{+})
as the value of the
plausibility
of
\phi
(recall Lemma
2.11
), we have that the following equivalence is valid:

\mathsf{Pl}\phi\leftrightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{B}\neg\phi

Definition 4.26

(Belief function axioms)
.

We define a sequence of outer formulas
\gamma_{n}

in propositional letters of the inner language
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
inductively as follows:

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathsf{B}p_{1}

\displaystyle\gamma_{n+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\gamma_{n}\oplus(\mathsf{B}p_{n+1}\ominus\gamma_{n}[\mathsf{B}\psi:\mathsf{B}(\psi\wedge p_{n+1})\mid\mathsf{B}\psi\mbox{ atoms of }\gamma_{n}])

where
\gamma_{n}[\mathsf{B}\psi:\mathsf{B}(\psi\wedge p_{n+1})\mid\mathsf{B}\psi\mbox{ modal atoms of }\gamma_{n}])
is the result of replacing each modal atom
\mathsf{B}\psi
in
\gamma_{n}
with the modal atom
\mathsf{B}(\psi\wedge p_{n+1})
(semantically, it is a relativisation of the corresponding belief function to the sets
|p_{n+1}|^{+-}
).

The
n
-th belief function axiom (i.e. the
n
-monotonicity) is expressed by substitution instances (substituting inner formulas for the atomic letters
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
) of

\alpha_{n}\coloneqq\gamma_{n}\rightarrow\mathsf{B}\left(\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}p_{n}\right).

Lemma 4.27

(Soundness of the axioms)
.

The modal axioms
\alpha_{n}
are sound w.r.t.
\mathsf{DS}
models.

Proof.

We need to prove that the axioms introduced in Definition
4.26
are sound, i.e. that the formulas
\gamma_{n}
indeed express semantically the appropriate sums from Definition
2.6
. Because of the two-dimensional nature of the semantics of the modality

v^{\mathscr{M}}(\mathsf{B}\phi)=(v^{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\mathsf{B}\phi),v^{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(\mathsf{B}\phi))\coloneqq(\mathtt{bel}(|\phi|^{+}),\mathtt{bel}(|\phi|^{-})),

we need to consider both
\mathtt{bel}^{+}
as a general belief function on the Lindenbaum algebra
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
, and
\mathtt{bel}^{-}
as a general belief function on its dual
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}^{op}
.

Recall that
\mathtt{bel}^{+}
on
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
is
n
-monotone
if, for every
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}
, it holds that

\mathtt{bel}^{+}\left(\bigvee\limits^{n}_{i=1}a_{i}\right)\geq\sum\limits_{\scriptsize{\begin{matrix}J\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}\\
J\neq\varnothing\end{matrix}}}(-1)^{|J|+1}\cdot\mathtt{bel}^{+}\left(\bigwedge\limits_{j\in J}a_{j}\right).

What the sum expresses is that we always add (
+
) beliefs of conjunctions of an odd size, while we subtract (
-
) beliefs of conjunctions of an even size. To write down exactly the same sum as a
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
formula is however more problematic than in the probability case because the sums and subtractions of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
are truncated to stay within the margins of
[0,1]
and we have to be careful to reflect this in the way we arrange the elements of the sum so that we never lose information. We first need to see that the axioms can indeed be re-defined recursively as follows (
\mathtt{t}_{n}
now denote terms in the language of linear inequalities):

\displaystyle\mathtt{t}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathtt{bel}^{+}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\mathtt{t}_{n+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathtt{t}_{n}+(\mathtt{bel}^{+}(a_{n+1})-\mathtt{t}_{n}[\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\psi):\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\psi\wedge a_{n+1})\mid\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\psi)\ \mbox{atoms of}\ \mathtt{t}_{n}])

The
n
-th axiom is now written as (the trivial case of
n=1
is included for convenience):

\mathtt{t}_{n}\leq\mathtt{bel}^{+}\left(\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}a_{n}\right).

The intuition behind this definition comes from the powerset algebras and it is geometrical. In each inductive step, we look at what happens if we add one more set (element
a_{n+1}
) to the union: we keep the sum we had so far (
\mathtt{t}_{n}
), add
\mathtt{bel}(a_{n+1})
, and subtract the sum we had
relativised
to
\mathtt{bel}(a_{n+1})
(replacing beliefs of the sets in the original sum by beliefs of their intersection with the new set
a_{n+1}
). That the two definitions are equivalent can be proven by a routine (but tedious) induction. The whole point of this definition is that in the above,

\mathtt{bel}^{+}(a_{n+1})\geq\mathtt{t}_{n}[\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\psi):\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\psi\wedge a_{n+1})\mid\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\psi)\ \mbox{atoms of}\ \mathtt{t}_{n}],

and therefore

0\leq\mathtt{bel}^{+}(a_{n+1})-\mathtt{t}_{n}[\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\psi):\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\psi\wedge a_{n+1})\mid\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\psi)\ \mbox{atoms of}\ \mathtt{t}_{n}]\leq 1.

Dually, recall that as
\mathtt{bel}^{-}
is a general belief function on
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}^{op}
, then for every
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}
it holds that

\mathtt{bel}^{-}\left(\bigwedge\limits^{n}_{i=1}a_{i}\right)\leq\sum\limits_{\scriptsize{\begin{matrix}J\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}\\
J\neq\varnothing\end{matrix}}}(-1)^{|J|+1}\cdot\mathtt{bel}^{-}\left(\bigvee\limits_{j\in J}a_{j}\right).

These axioms can be re-defined recursively as follows (this is tailored to be expressed in the Łukasiewicz language):

\displaystyle\mathtt{s}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathtt{bel}^{-}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\mathtt{s}_{n+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(\mathtt{s}_{n}+1-\mathtt{s}_{n}[\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\psi):\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\psi\vee a_{n+1})\mid\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\psi)\ \mbox{atoms of}\ \mathtt{s}_{n}])-(1-\mathtt{bel}^{-}(a_{n+1})).

The
n
-th axiom is now written as:

\mathtt{s}_{n}\geq\mathtt{bel}^{-}\left(\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}a_{n}\right).

The main reason we did it this particular way is that now the following sum stays within margins of
[0,1]
(and therefore directly corresponds to a
Ł
formula):

0\leq\mathtt{s}_{n}+1-\mathtt{s}_{n}[\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\psi):\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\psi\vee a_{n+1})\mid\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\psi)\ \mbox{atoms of}\ \mathtt{s}_{n}]\leq 1.

It remains to show that for each
n
and each
\mathsf{DS}
model
(S,v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{bel})
we will have

v_{1}(\gamma_{n})\leq\mathtt{bel}\left|\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}p_{n}\right|^{+}\mbox{ and}\quad v_{2}(\gamma_{n})\geq\mathtt{bel}\left|\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}p_{n}\right|^{-}.

The case
n=1
is trivial, we, therefore, go directly for the recursion step. For the first coordinate,

\displaystyle v_{1}(\gamma_{n+1})

\displaystyle=v_{1}(\gamma_{n})\oplus_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}(\mathtt{bel}(|p_{n+1}|^{+})\ominus_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}v_{1}(\gamma_{n}[\mathsf{B}\psi:\mathsf{B}(\psi\wedge p_{n+1})]))

\displaystyle=\mathtt{t}_{n}(|p_{1}|^{+},\ldots,|p_{n}|^{+})+(\mathtt{bel}(|p_{n+1}|^{+})-\mathtt{t}_{n}[\mathtt{bel}(|\psi|^{+}):\mathtt{bel}(|\psi\wedge p_{n+1}|^{+})])

\displaystyle=\mathtt{t}_{n}+(\mathtt{bel}(|p_{n+1}|^{+})-\mathtt{t}_{n}[\mathtt{bel}(|\psi|^{+}):\mathtt{bel}(|\psi|^{+}\cap|p_{n+1}|^{+})])

\displaystyle\leq\mathtt{bel}\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}|p_{n}|^{+}=\mathtt{bel}\left|\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}p_{n}\right|^{+}.

For the second coordinate,

\displaystyle v_{2}(\gamma_{n+1})

\displaystyle=v_{2}(\gamma_{n})~\&_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}~(v_{2}(\gamma_{n}[\mathsf{B}\psi:\mathsf{B}(\psi\wedge p_{n+1})])\rightarrow_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}\mathtt{bel}(|p_{n+1}|^{-}))

\displaystyle=(v_{2}(\gamma_{n})\oplus_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}{\sim_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}v_{2}(\gamma_{n}[\mathsf{B}\psi:\mathsf{B}(\psi\wedge p_{n+1})]))\ominus_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}{\sim_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\mathtt{bel}(|p_{n+1}|^{-})

\displaystyle=(\mathtt{s}_{n}(|p_{1}|^{-},\ldots,|p_{n}|^{-})+1-\mathtt{s}_{n}[\mathtt{bel}(|\psi|^{-}):\mathtt{bel}(|\psi\vee p_{n+1}|^{-})])-(1-\mathtt{bel}(|p_{n+1}|^{-}))

\displaystyle=(\mathtt{s}_{n}+1-\mathtt{s}_{n}[\mathtt{bel}(|\psi|^{-}):\mathtt{bel}(|\psi|^{-}\cap|p_{n+1}|^{-})])-(1-\mathtt{bel}(|p_{n+1}|^{-}))

\displaystyle\geq\mathtt{bel}\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\left|p_{n}\right|^{-}=\mathtt{bel}\left|\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}p_{n}\right|^{-}

∎

Definition 4.28

(Axiomatisation of
\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
)
.

The two-layered axiomatisation of logic
\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}=(\mathsf{BD},M_{b},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2})
consists of

inner logic:

the axiomatisation of
\mathsf{BD}
of Definition
2.1

modal axioms:

M_{b}
:

\displaystyle\{\mathsf{B}\varphi

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathsf{B}\psi\mid\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}\neg\varphi

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\neg\mathsf{B}\varphi

\displaystyle\{\alpha_{n}\

\displaystyle|\ n\in\mathbb{N}\}\mbox{ (substitution instances of)}

outer logic:

the axiomatisation of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
of Definition
4.12

A proof of a formula
\alpha
from a set of assumptions
\Gamma
in the logic
\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
(in short
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
), where
\Gamma,\alpha
is a finite set of outer formulas, is a finite tree which can be parsed as follows: the smallest proofs consist of either (i) a finite tree labeled by
\mathsf{BD}
formulas, proving
\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi
, followed by the instance
\mathsf{B}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{B}\psi
of the first modal axiom, or (ii) a node labelled by an instance of the remaining two modal axioms or an instance of an axiom of the outer logic in the outer language, or (iii) an assumption
\beta\in\Gamma
in the outer language. More complex proofs are then formed inductively in the standard manner, by means of syntactically correct applications of the rules of the outer logic to smaller proofs. The root of the tree is to be labelled by
\alpha
.

Theorem 4.29

(FSSC of
\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
)
.

For each
\Gamma,\alpha
a finite set of outer formulas,

\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha\ \ \mbox{iff}\ \ \Gamma\vDash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha.

Proof.

The soundness and completeness proof essentially relies on the two-layered nature of the logic. We will use the following facts: (i) the inner logic
\mathsf{BD}
is strongly sound and complete w.r.t. its double-valuation semantics, and it is locally finite, (ii) the outer logic
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
is finitely strongly sound and complete w.r.t. the outer algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
, and (iii) the modal axioms are a sound and complete axiomatisation of belief functions over
\mathsf{DS}
models.

Soundness
: Given a proof
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
and any
\mathsf{DS}
model
\mathscr{M}=(S,v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{bel})
, we reason inductively: (i) we know that any proof of
\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi
is sound, therefore it entails that
|\varphi|^{+}\subseteq|\psi|^{+}
and
|\psi|^{-}\subseteq|\varphi|^{-}
in any
\mathsf{BD}
model, and thus in
\mathscr{M}
. This entails that
\mathtt{bel}^{+}\varphi\leq\mathtt{bel}^{+}\psi
and
\mathtt{bel}^{-}\psi\leq\mathtt{bel}^{-}\varphi
, and the following instance
\mathsf{B}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{B}\psi
of the first modal axiom is justified, as it results in the value
(1,0)
in the outer algebra. The rest of the proof follows from the soundness of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
, providing the rest of the modal axioms are sound. Assume an instance
\mathsf{B}\neg\varphi\leftrightarrow\neg\mathsf{B}\varphi
of the second modal axiom: Its left-hand side
\mathsf{B}\neg\varphi
amounts to
(\mathtt{bel}|\neg\varphi|^{+},\mathtt{bel}|\neg\varphi|^{-})=(\mathtt{bel}|\varphi|^{-},\mathtt{bel}|\varphi|^{+})
which is the semantics of
\neg\mathsf{B}\varphi
as desired. The soundness of the axioms
\alpha_{n}
has been demonstrated in Lemma
4.27
.

Completeness
: Assume that
\Gamma\nvdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
. We will show that there is
\mathsf{DS}
model validating
\Gamma
while refuting
\alpha
. We will again use the canonical model
\mathscr{M}_{c}=(\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{Lit}),v_{c}^{+},v_{c}^{-})
of Definition
3.5
to do so.
Consider the finite set of
\mathsf{BD}
subformulas of modal atoms of
\Gamma,\Phi
to be given by

\mathtt{Sf}_{\Gamma,\alpha}\coloneqq\{\varphi\mid\exists\varphi^{\prime}\ \mathsf{B}\varphi^{\prime}\mbox{ a modal atom of }\Gamma\cup\{\alpha\}\mbox{ and }\varphi\mbox{ a subformula of }\varphi^{\prime}\}.

Because
\mathsf{BD}
is locally finite, also the set

\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}\coloneqq\{\psi\mid\exists\varphi\in\mathtt{Sf}_{\Gamma,\alpha}\ \mbox{ and }\ \varphi\dashv\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi\}

is finite. Now define the finite set
\Sigma
of outer formulas to contain all instances of the modal axioms for formulas from
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
.

Given that
\Gamma\nvdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
, it is obviously also the case that
\Gamma,\Sigma\nvdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\alpha
.
Then we know, by FSSC of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
, that there is a refuting valuation

v:\{\mathsf{B}\varphi\mid\varphi\in\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}\}\to[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}},

i.e.
v[\Gamma\cup\Sigma]\subseteq\{(1,0)\}
while
v(\alpha)<(1,0)
. Because
v[\Sigma]\subseteq\{(1,0)\}
, and
\Sigma
in fact fully describes behaviour of a belief function on the
\mathsf{BD}
formula algebra generated by propositional atoms from the set
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
, we define the belief function of a
\mathsf{BD}
formula
\phi\in\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
to be

(\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\varphi),\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\varphi))=v(\mathsf{B}\varphi).

It is then a routine to check that this assignment indeed is a belief function on the respective formula algebra (or its dual). Therefore, by Theorem
3.12
, we know that this map can be extended to a belief function
\mathtt{bel}^{\prime}
on the powerset of the canonical
\mathsf{BD}
model

(\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{Lit}),v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{bel}^{\prime})
with
\mathsf{Lit}
being the finite set of literals from the set
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
, i.e.
\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\varphi)=\mathtt{bel}^{\prime}(|\varphi|^{+})
and
\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\varphi)=\mathtt{bel}^{\prime}(|\varphi|^{-})
for each formula from the set
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Example 4.4
.

Assume that
\mathsf{B}\varphi
is classical, i.e. that
\mathsf{B}\varphi\leftrightarrow{\sim}\neg\mathsf{B}\varphi
. Then, starting from the
\alpha_{2}
axiom, and
\mathsf{B}(\varphi\vee\neg\varphi)\leftrightarrow\neg\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)
, we obtain, consequently using the conflation rule and distribution, the classicality, contraposition,
\vee
-definition, and finally
\mathsf{B}(\varphi\vee\neg\varphi)\vee\mathsf{B}\neg\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathsf{B}(\varphi\vee\neg\varphi)
:

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}((\mathsf{B}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi))\rightarrow\mathsf{B}\neg\varphi)\rightarrow\neg\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}{\sim}\neg((\mathsf{B}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi))\rightarrow\mathsf{B}\neg\varphi)\rightarrow\neg\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}((\mathsf{B}\varphi\rightarrow{\sim}\neg\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi))\rightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{B}\varphi)\rightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}((\neg\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)\rightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{B}\varphi)\rightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{B}\varphi)\rightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}(\neg\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)\vee{\sim}\mathsf{B}\varphi)\rightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}(\mathsf{B}(\varphi\vee\neg\varphi)\vee\neg\mathsf{B}\varphi)\rightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)

\displaystyle\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}}\mathsf{B}(\varphi\vee\neg\varphi)\rightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{B}(\varphi\wedge\neg\varphi)

The converse implication is provable as well. (Also note that the same proof with
\mathsf{B}
replaced by
\mathsf{Pr}
would work for probabilities, thus extending the Example
4.3
).

4.4.2
Two-layered logic for belief and plausibility functions

Definition 4.30

(Language and semantics of
\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
)
.

The two-layered language of the logic
\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
is of the form
(\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}},\{\mathsf{B},\mathsf{Pl}\},\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}})
, with unary modalities
\mathsf{B}
and
\mathsf{Pl}
, and it is defined as follows:

inner formulas:

formulas of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}

atomic modal formulas:

Let
\phi\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
, then
\mathsf{B}\phi
and
\mathsf{Pl}\phi
are modal atomic formulas.

outer formulas:

Let
\mathsf{B}\phi
and
\mathsf{Pl}\phi
range over modal atomic formulas, the set of outer formulas is defined via the following grammar, with the connectives of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
:

\alpha\coloneqq\mathsf{B}\phi\mid\mathsf{Pl}\phi\mid\neg\alpha\mid\alpha\wedge\alpha\mid\alpha\rightarrowtriangle\alpha\mid{\sim}\alpha.

Other connectives:
\bot
,
\vee
,
\&
,
\ominus
and
\oplus
, can be defined as in
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
of Definition
4.17
.

The semantics of the two-layered language is given over a
\mathsf{DS}_{\mathtt{pl}}
model
\mathscr{M}=(W,v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{bel},\mathtt{pl})
, and the outer algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
, as follows:

inner formulas:

for each
\phi\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
the model provides sets
|\phi|^{+}=\{w\mid w\vDash^{+}\phi\}
and
|\phi|^{-}=\{w\mid w\vDash^{-}\phi\}
, as described in Definition
2.2
.

atomic modal formulas:

for atomic modal formulas
\mathsf{B}\phi
and
\mathsf{Pl}\phi
,
v^{\mathscr{M}}(\mathsf{B}\phi)
and
v^{\mathscr{M}}(\mathsf{Pl}\phi)
in
[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
are defined as follows:

v^{\mathscr{M}}(\mathsf{B}\phi)=(v^{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\mathsf{B}\phi),v^{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(\mathsf{B}\phi))\coloneqq(\mathtt{bel}(|\phi|^{+}),\mathtt{pl}(|\phi|^{-}));

v^{\mathscr{M}}(\mathsf{Pl}\phi)=(v^{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\mathsf{Pl}\phi),v^{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(\mathsf{Pl}\phi))\coloneqq(\mathtt{pl}(|\phi|^{+}),\mathtt{bel}(|\phi|^{-})).

outer formulas:

The map
v^{\mathscr{M}}
is extended to the outer formulas in an expected manner, following the Definition
4.11
of the interpretation of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
connectives in the algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
.

The outer formula
\alpha
is said to be valid in
\mathscr{M}
, if
v^{\mathscr{M}}(\alpha)
is designated (i.e.
v_{1}^{\mathscr{M}}(\alpha)=1
). It is said to be valid if it is valid in all
\mathsf{DS}_{\mathtt{pl}}
models.
The consequence relation for
\Gamma,\alpha
a set of outer formulas is defined as

\Gamma\vDash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\alpha\ \ \mbox{iff}\ \ \forall\mathscr{M}(v_{1}^{\mathscr{M}}[\Gamma]\subseteq\{1\}\Rightarrow v_{1}^{\mathscr{M}}(\alpha)=1).

Definition 4.31

(Belief and plausibility functions axioms)
.

We define sequences of outer formulas
\gamma_{n}
, and
\sigma_{n}
in propositional letters of the inner language
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
inductively as follows:

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathsf{B}p_{1}

\displaystyle\gamma_{n+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\gamma_{n}\oplus(\mathsf{B}p_{n+1}\ominus\gamma_{n}[\mathsf{B}\psi:\mathsf{B}(\psi\wedge p_{n+1})\mid\mathsf{B}\psi\mbox{ atoms of }\gamma_{n}])

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathsf{Pl}p_{1}

\displaystyle\sigma_{n+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(\sigma_{n}\oplus{\sim}\sigma_{n}[\mathsf{Pl}\psi:\mathsf{Pl}(\psi\vee p_{n+1})\mid\mathsf{Pl}\psi\mbox{ atoms of }\sigma_{n}])\ominus{\sim}\mathsf{Pl}p_{n+1}

where
\gamma_{n}[\mathsf{B}\psi:\mathsf{B}(\psi\wedge p_{n+1})\mid\mathsf{B}\psi\mbox{ atoms of }\gamma_{n}])
is the result of replacing each atom
\mathsf{B}\psi
in
\gamma_{n}
with the atom
\mathsf{B}(\psi\wedge p_{n+1})
, and
\sigma_{n}[\mathsf{Pl}\psi:\mathsf{Pl}(\psi\vee p_{n+1})\mid\mathsf{Pl}\psi\mbox{ atoms of }\sigma_{n}]
is the result of replacing each atom
\mathsf{Pl}\psi
in
\sigma_{n}
with the atom
\mathsf{Pl}(\psi\vee p_{n+1})
.

The
n
-th belief function axiom (i.e. the
n
-monotonicity) is expressed by substitution instances (substituting inner formulas for the atomic letters
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
) of

\alpha_{n}\coloneqq\gamma_{n}\rightarrowtriangle\mathsf{B}\left(\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}p_{n}\right).

The
n
-th plausibility function axiom is expressed by substitution instances (substituting inner formulas for the atomic letters
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
) of

\beta_{n}\coloneqq\mathsf{Pl}\left(\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}p_{n}\right)\rightarrowtriangle\sigma_{n}.

Lemma 4.32

(Soundness of the axioms)
.

The axioms
\alpha_{n}
and
\beta_{n}
are sound w.r.t.
\mathsf{DS}_{\mathtt{pl}}
models.

Proof.

We need to prove that the axioms introduced in Definition
4.31
are sound, i.e. that the formulas
\gamma_{n},\sigma_{n}
indeed express semantically the appropriate sums from Definition
2.6
and Definition
2.7
. First note that the validity of the axioms
\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}
means that, for each
\mathsf{DS}_{\mathtt{pl}}
model
\mathscr{M}
,
v^{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\alpha_{n})=1
and
v^{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\beta_{n})=1
. Thus we only care about the first coordinates. Because the weak implication still expresses the order of the first coordinates (
(a_{1},a_{2})\rightarrowtriangle(b_{1},b_{2})
is designated if and only if
a_{1}\leq b_{1}
), we only need to check that the first coordinates of formulas
\gamma_{n},\sigma_{n}
express semantically the appropriate sums from Definition
2.6
and Definition
2.7
. But this can be done exactly as in proof of Lemma
4.27
, using the terms
\mathtt{t}_{n}
for
\gamma_{n}
, and straightforward analogues of terms
\mathtt{s}_{n}
for
\sigma_{n}
. This follows from the fact, that the
n
-th axiom of the general belief function
\mathtt{bel}^{-}
on
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}^{op}
and the
n
-th axiom of the general plausibility function
\mathtt{pl}^{+}
on
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
are of the same shape (not to confuse the two notions however, notice that while
\mathtt{bel}^{-}
is antitone on
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
,
\mathtt{pl}^{+}
is monotone on
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
).
∎

Definition 4.33

(Axiomatisation of
\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
)
.

The two-layered axiomatisation of logic
\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}=(\mathsf{BD},M^{\mathbb{N}}_{b},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}_{(1,1)^{\uparrow}}(\rightarrowtriangle))

inner logic:

the axiomatisation of
\mathsf{BD}
of Definition
2.1

modal axioms:

M_{b}^{\mathbb{N}}
:

\displaystyle\{\mathsf{B}\varphi

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\mathsf{B}\psi,\mathsf{Pl}\varphi\Rightarrow\mathsf{Pl}\psi\mid\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}\neg\varphi

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\neg\mathsf{Pl}\varphi

\displaystyle\{\alpha_{n},\beta_{n}

\displaystyle\mid n\in\mathbb{N}\}\mbox{ (substitution instances of)}

outer logic:

the axiomatisation of
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
of Definition
4.18

A proof of a formula
\alpha
from a set of assumptions
\Gamma
in the logic
\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
(in short
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\alpha
), where
\Gamma,\alpha
is a finite set of outer formulas, is a finite tree which can be parsed as follows: the smallest proofs consist of either (i) a finite tree labeled by
\mathsf{BD}
formulas, proving
\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi
, followed by the instance
\mathsf{B}\varphi\Rightarrow\mathsf{B}\psi
or by the instance
\mathsf{Pl}\varphi\Rightarrow\mathsf{Pl}\psi
of the first modal axiom, or (ii) a node labelled by an instance of the remaining two modal axioms or an instance of an axiom of the outer logic in the outer language, or (iii) an assumption
\beta\in\Gamma
in the outer language. More complex proofs are then formed inductively in the standard manner, by means of syntactically correct applications of the rules of the outer logic to smaller proofs. The root of the tree is to be labelled by
\alpha
.

Theorem 4.34

(FSSC of
\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
)
.

For each
\Gamma,\alpha
a finite set of outer formulas,

\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\alpha\ \ \mbox{iff}\ \ \Gamma\vDash_{\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\alpha.

Proof.

The soundness and completeness proof is almost the same as before. We will now use the following facts: (i) the inner logic
\mathsf{BD}
is strongly sound and complete w.r.t. its double-valuation semantics, and it is locally finite, (ii) the outer logic
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
is finitely strongly sound and complete w.r.t. the outer algebra
[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
, and (iii) the modal axioms are a sound and complete axiomatisation of belief and plausibility functions over
\mathsf{DS}_{\mathtt{pl}}
models.

Soundness
: Given a proof
\Gamma\vdash_{\mathtt{Bel}^{N{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\alpha
and any
\mathsf{DS}_{\mathtt{pl}}
model
\mathscr{M}=(S,v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{bel},\mathtt{pl})
, we reason inductively: (i) we know that any proof of
\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi
is sound, therefore it entails that
|\varphi|^{+}\subseteq|\psi|^{+}
and
|\psi|^{-}\subseteq|\varphi|^{-}
in any
\mathsf{BD}
model, and thus in
\mathscr{M}
. This entails that
\mathtt{bel}(|\varphi|^{+})\leq\mathtt{bel}(|\psi|^{+})
and
\mathtt{bel}(|\psi|^{+})\leq\mathtt{bel}(|\varphi|^{+})
, and
\mathtt{pl}(|\varphi|^{+})\leq\mathtt{pl}(|\psi|^{+})
and
\mathtt{pl}(|\psi|^{-})\leq\mathtt{pl}(|\varphi|^{-})
, and either of the following instances
\mathsf{B}\varphi\Rightarrow\mathsf{B}\psi
and
\mathsf{Pl}\varphi\Rightarrow\mathsf{Pl}\psi
of the first modal axiom is justified, as it results in a value
(1,x)
in the outer algebra. The rest of the proof follows from the soundness of
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
, providing the rest of the modal axioms are sound. Assume an instance
\mathsf{B}\neg\varphi\Leftrightarrow\neg\mathsf{Pl}\varphi
of the second modal axiom: its left-hand side
\mathsf{B}\neg\varphi
amounts to
(\mathtt{bel}(|\neg\varphi|^{+}),\mathtt{pl}(|\neg\varphi|^{-}))=(\mathtt{bel}(|\varphi|^{-}),\mathtt{pl}(|\varphi|^{+}))
which matches exactly the semantics of
\neg\mathsf{Pl}\varphi
as desired. The soundness of the axioms
\alpha_{n}
, and
\beta_{n}
has been demonstrated in Lemma
4.32
.

Completeness
: Assume that
\Gamma\nvdash_{\mathtt{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\alpha
. We will show that there is
\mathsf{DS}_{\mathtt{pl}}
model validating
\Gamma
while refuting
\alpha
. We will again use the canonical model
\mathscr{M}_{c}=(\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{Lit}),v_{c}^{+},v_{c}^{-})
of Definition
3.5
to do so. Consider the finite set of
\mathsf{BD}
subformulas of modal atoms of
\Gamma,\Phi
to be given by

\mathtt{Sf}_{\Gamma,\alpha}\coloneqq\{\varphi\mid\exists\mathsf{M}\!\in\!\{\mathsf{B},\mathsf{Pl}\}~\exists\varphi^{\prime}:\mathsf{M}\varphi^{\prime}\mbox{ a modal atom of }\Gamma\cup\{\alpha\}\mbox{ and }\varphi\mbox{ a subformula of }\varphi^{\prime}\}.

Because
\mathsf{BD}
is locally finite, also the set

\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}\coloneqq\{\psi\mid\exists\varphi\in\mathtt{Sf}_{\Gamma,\alpha}\ \mbox{ and }\ \varphi\dashv\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi)\}

is finite. Now define the finite set
\Sigma
of outer formulas to contain all instances of the modal axioms for formulas from
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
.

Given that
\Gamma\nvdash_{\mathtt{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\alpha
, it is obviously also the case that
\Gamma,\Sigma\nvdash_{\mathtt{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}}\alpha
.
Then we know, by FSSC of
\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}
, that there is a refuting valuation

v:\{\mathsf{B}\varphi\mid\varphi\in\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}\}\to[0,1]^{\Join}_{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}},

i.e.
v_{1}[\Gamma\cup\Sigma]\subseteq\{1\}
while
v_{1}(\alpha)<1
. Because
v_{1}[\Sigma]\subseteq\{1\}
, and
\Sigma
in fact fully describes behaviour of a belief or plausibility function on the
\mathsf{BD}
formula algebra generated by propositional atoms from the set
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
, we can define the belief functions
\mathtt{bel}^{+},\mathtt{bel}^{-}
and the plausibility functions
\mathtt{pl}^{+},\mathtt{pl}^{-}
of a
\mathsf{BD}
formula
\varphi\in\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
to be

\displaystyle\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\varphi)=v_{1}(\mathsf{B}\varphi)

\displaystyle\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\varphi)=v_{2}(\mathsf{Pl}\varphi)

\displaystyle\mathtt{pl}^{+}(\varphi)=v_{1}(\mathsf{Pl}\varphi)

\displaystyle\mathtt{pl}^{-}(\varphi)=v_{2}(\mathsf{B}\varphi).

It is then a routine to check that this assignment indeed results in belief and plausibility functions on the respective formula algebra (or its dual).

Therefore, by Theorem
3.12
and Theorem
3.13
, we know that
\mathtt{bel}
can be extended to a belief function
\mathtt{bel}^{\prime}
, and
\mathtt{pl}
to
\mathtt{pl}^{\prime}
, on the powerset of the canonical
\mathsf{BD}
model

(\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{Lit}),v^{+},v^{-},\mathtt{bel}^{\prime},\mathtt{pl}^{\prime})
with
\mathsf{Lit}
being the finite set of literals from the set
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
, i.e.
\mathtt{bel}^{+}(\varphi)=\mathtt{bel}^{\prime}(|\varphi|^{+})
,
\mathtt{bel}^{-}(\varphi)=\mathtt{bel}^{\prime}(|\varphi|^{-})
,
\mathtt{pl}^{+}(\varphi)=\mathtt{pl}^{\prime}(|\varphi|^{+})
and
\mathtt{pl}^{-}(\varphi)=\mathtt{pl}^{\prime}(|\varphi|^{-})
for each formula from the set
\Phi_{\Gamma,\alpha}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.8

(Belief below plausibility)
.

When dealing with
\mathsf{DS}_{\mathtt{pl}}
models in the context of Subsection
3.3.3
where
\mathtt{bel}(X)\leq\mathtt{pl}(X)
for any
X\subseteq\mathcal{P}(S)
, we simply extend the logic
\mathsf{Bel}^{\mathsf{N}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}}
with additional axioms

\mathsf{B}\varphi\rightarrowtriangle\mathsf{Pl}\varphi.

Note that
\mathsf{B}\varphi\rightarrowtriangle\mathsf{Pl}\varphi
proves
\neg\mathsf{Pl}\varphi\rightarrowtriangle\neg\mathsf{B}\varphi
because of the
\mathsf{B}\neg
-axiom. The additional axioms are sound, whenever we ensure that
\mathtt{bel}(|\varphi|^{+})\leq\mathtt{pl}(|\varphi|^{+})
in the underlying
\mathsf{DS}_{\mathtt{pl}}
models.

We conclude this whole subsection with some general remarks concerning the two-layered Łukasiewicz-based logics presented above.

Remark 4.9

(Adding
\bot
and
\top
to the inner logic)
.

Adding the constants
\bot
and
\top
to
\mathsf{BD}
results in the logic
\mathsf{BD}^{*}
(see Subsection
2.2
for its axiomatisation). Using
\mathsf{BD}^{*}
as the inner logic, however, affects how the modal part of the logic is to be completely axiomatised because we now have formulas whose measure is always
0
(or
1
respectively). This is simply achieved by extending the modal part with the modal axiom
\mathsf{Pr}\top
(respectively,
\mathsf{B}\top

\mathsf{Pl}\top
). One can think of it as a way to avoid a necessitation modal rule, which would only make sense to add if we allowed inner formulas as assumptions in the consequence relation of the two-layered logics (see Remark
4.11
).

Remark 4.10
.

As one can see from Remark
4.9
,
\mathsf{Pr}
,
\mathsf{B}
, and
\mathsf{Pl}
are not
normal
modalities (unless the inner logic includes constants) in the sense that no formula of the form
\mathsf{M}\phi
with
\phi\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
and
\mathsf{M}\in\{\mathsf{Pr},\mathsf{B},\mathsf{Pl}\}
is valid. Note, however, that all three modalities are
regular
: if
\phi\vdash\chi
is
\mathsf{BD}
- or
\mathsf{BD}^{*}
-valid, then
\mathsf{M}\phi\rightsquigarrow\mathsf{M}\chi
is valid as well for
\rightsquigarrow\in\{\rightarrow,\rightarrowtriangle,\Rightarrow\}
.

Remark 4.11

(Allowing inner assumptions)
.

If we wish to allow inner formulas as assumptions in the consequence relation of the two-layered logics (in line with the abstract approach of
[
12
,
3
]
), we would consider a mixed-consequence relation of the form

\Phi,\Gamma\vdash\alpha,

where
\Phi
is a finite set of inner formulas, and
\Gamma,\alpha
is a finite set of outer formulas. The semantics is now given as follows: for each model
\mathscr{M}=(S,v^{+},v^{-},f)
validating
\Phi
(in the sense of
\mathsf{BD}
semantics), if the
f
-derived valuation
v^{\mathscr{M}}
validates
\Gamma
in the outer algebra, it also validates
\alpha
. For the axiomatisation, we need to update it with the following (obviously sound)
modal rules
, i.e. rules whose assumptions are inner formulas and whose conclusion is an outer formula (replacing now redundant monotonicity modal axioms):

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle\lhd\mathsf{B}\varphi

\displaystyle\{\Phi

\displaystyle\lhd\mathsf{B}\varphi\rightarrow\mathsf{B}\psi\mid\Phi,\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi\}

and similarly for the other modalities. The notion of a two-layered proof needs to be adapted as well in an expected manner: upper parts of a proof tree may consist of a
\mathsf{BD}
proof followed by an application of a modal rule. The completeness proof becomes more subtle now as well: basically, to refute
\Phi,\Gamma\vdash\alpha
, we need to construct an appropriate measure on the canonical
\mathsf{BD}
model for the finite set
\mathsf{Lit}
of literals from
\Phi,\Gamma\vdash\alpha
, relativized to
\Phi
(i.e.,
(\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{Lit}),v^{+}_{c},v^{-}_{c})
, relativized to the set
|\bigwedge\Phi|^{+}
). This takes into account that we need to measure
\mathsf{BD}
formulas up to equi-provability from
\Phi
13
13

13

It is worth mentioning that the relation
\varphi\equiv_{\Phi}\psi
given as
\Phi,\varphi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\psi
and
\Phi,\psi\vdash_{\mathsf{BD}}\varphi
is in general not a congruence w.r.t.
\neg
. The logic
\mathsf{BD}
is only weakly Fregean.
.

4.5
Translations

Here, we follow
[
3
]
and provide a translation of the two-layered logics with inequalities given in section
4.1
into
\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
and
\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}
and back. In fact, the original translations work just fine.

4.5.1
Translations to
\mathsf{Pr}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}

Definition 4.35

(Functional counterparts)
.

Let
l_{1}
, …,
l_{n}
be literals, and
\alpha(l_{1},\ldots,l_{n})
be an
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}_{\triangle^{\top}}
formula in NNF. A
functional counterpart
of
\alpha
(denoted
f_{\alpha}
) is a function
f_{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
s.t. for any
v
,

\displaystyle v_{1}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=f_{\alpha}(v_{1}(l_{1}),\ldots,v_{1}(l_{n}))

\displaystyle v_{2}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=f_{\alpha}(v_{2}(l_{1}),\ldots,v_{2}(l_{n}))

Definition 4.36

(From weight formulas to Łukasiewicz two-layer formulas)
.

Let

\underbrace{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\mathtt{w}^{+}(\phi_{i})}_{t}\geqslant c

be a PWF that does not contain
\mathtt{w}^{-}
. Let, further, for any
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
f^{\sharp}=\min(1,\max(f,0))
.

Now, for

f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\mathtt{w}^{\pm}(\phi_{i})-c+1

let
\beta(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})
be an
Ł
-formula s.t.
f_{\beta}=f^{\sharp}
. Thus, we can define the
∙
-translation as follows
14
14

14

Recall that we manipulate with weight formulas classically.
.

\displaystyle(t\geqslant c)^{\bullet}

\displaystyle={\triangle^{\top}}\beta(\mathsf{Pr}\phi_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{Pr}\phi_{n})

\displaystyle\bot^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=\bot

\displaystyle\alpha\supset\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{\bullet}\rightarrow_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}\alpha^{\prime\bullet}

Remark 4.12
.

Observe that we can actually avoid using
\neg
if we use
\sim
or
\bot
as a primitive
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
-connective. This will give us an embedding into
\mathsf{Pr_{lin}}
from
[
3
]
. Note, furthermore, that because of
{\triangle^{\top}}
, all translated formulas have either value
(1,0)
, or
(0,1)
. Again, we do not actually use two-dimensionality. Furthermore, it is evident that all
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}_{(x,y)}
entailments are the same and that
\neg
and
\sim
are identical if all atomic propositions are prenexed by
{\triangle^{\top}}
.

Proposition 4.37
.

Let
\Xi\cup\{\alpha\}
be a set of weight formulas. Then,
\Xi\vDash_{\mathtt{m}\mathsf{BD}}\alpha
iff
\Xi^{\bullet}\vDash_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}\alpha^{\bullet}
.

We define our translation on NNFs of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
formulas. Note also that
\neg{\triangle^{\top}}\phi\leftrightarrow_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}{\sim}{\triangle^{\top}}\phi
. Thus, we can ignore the Belnap–Dunn negation.

Definition 4.38

(From Łukasiewicz two-layer formulas to weight formulas)
.

Let
\alpha
be an NNF of an
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
probabilistic formula and let
\alpha^{-}
be the result of replacing each
\mathsf{Pr}\phi_{i}
with a
p_{i}
. Since
f_{\alpha^{-}}=\max\limits_{k\in K}\min\limits_{j\in J_{k}}t_{k,j}
for some
K
and
J
where for every
k\in K
and
j_{k}\in J
there is a linear function
f_{k,j}
with integer coefficients and

t_{k,j}=f^{\sharp}_{k,j}\text{ or }t_{k,j}=1-{\triangle^{\top}}(1-f^{\sharp}_{f,j})

it is possible to define the
∘
-translation of
\alpha
as follows.

\alpha^{\circ}=\bigvee\limits_{k\in K}\bigwedge\limits_{j\in J_{k}}\alpha_{k,j}

Here for
f_{k,j}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot x_{i}+c
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{k,j}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\mathtt{w}^{+}(\phi_{i})\geqslant 1-c&\text{ if }t_{k,j}=f^{\sharp}_{k,j}\\
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\mathtt{w}^{+}(\phi_{i})<-c&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

Proposition 4.39
.

Let
\Xi\cup\{\alpha\}
be a set of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
two-layer formulas. Then,
\Xi\!\vDash_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}\!\alpha
iff
\Xi^{\circ}\!\vDash_{\mathtt{m}\mathsf{BD}}\!\alpha^{\circ}
.

4.5.2
Translations to
\mathsf{Bel}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}_{\triangle}

Definition 4.40

(Language and semantics of
\langle\mathsf{BD},B,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}_{(1,0)}(\rightarrow,\triangle)\rangle
for beliefs)
.

The language is as follows. Let
\phi\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{BD}}
. The set of (upper-layer) formulas is defined via the following grammar.

\alpha\coloneqq\mathtt{Bel}\phi\mid\neg\alpha\mid\alpha\rightarrow_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}\alpha\mid{\triangle^{\top}}\alpha

\bot
is defined as
\neg(\beta\rightarrow_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}\beta)
. All other connectives —
\sim
,
\wedge
,
\vee
,
\odot
, and
\oplus
— can be defined as usual.

The semantics is given over De Morgan algebras with belief functions:
v_{1}(\mathtt{Bel}\phi)=\mathtt{bel}(v(\phi))
;
v_{2}(\mathtt{Bel}\phi)=\mathtt{bel}(v(\neg\phi))
. The connectives are defined in the expected fashion.

Definition 4.41

(From belief formulas to Łukasiewicz modal formulas)
.

Let

\underbrace{\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\mathtt{b}^{+}(\phi_{i})}_{t}\geqslant c

be a PBF that does not contain
\mathtt{b}^{-}
. Let, further, for any
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
f^{\sharp}=\min(1,\max(f,0))
.

Now, for

f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\mathtt{w}^{\pm}(\phi_{i})-c+1

let
\beta(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})
be an
Ł
-formula s.t.
f_{\beta}=f^{\sharp}
(recall definition
4.35
). Thus, we can define the
∙
-translation as follows
15
15

15

Recall that we manipulate with weight formulas classically.
.

\displaystyle(t\geqslant c)^{\bullet}

\displaystyle={\triangle^{\top}}\beta(\mathsf{B}\phi_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{B}\phi_{n})

\displaystyle\bot^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=\bot

\displaystyle\alpha\supset\alpha^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{\bullet}\rightarrow_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}\alpha^{\prime\bullet}

Proposition 4.42
.

Let
\Xi\cup\{\alpha\}
be a set of belief formulas. Then,
\Xi\vDash_{\mathtt{b}\mathsf{BD}}\alpha
iff
\Xi^{\bullet}\vDash_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}\alpha^{\bullet}
.

Definition 4.43

(From Łukasiewicz two-layer formulas to belief formulas)
.

Let
\alpha
be an NNF of an
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
two-layer formula and let
\alpha^{-}
be the result of replacing each
\mathtt{Bel}\phi_{i}
with a
p_{i}
. Since
f_{\alpha^{-}}=\max\limits_{k\in K}\min\limits_{j\in J_{k}}t_{k,j}
for some
K
and
J
where for every
k\in K
and
j_{k}\in J
there is a linear function
f_{k,j}
with integer coefficients and

t_{k,j}=f^{\sharp}_{k,j}\text{ or }t_{k,j}=1-{\triangle^{\top}}(1-f^{\sharp}_{f,j})

it is possible to define the
∘
-translation of
\alpha
as follows

\alpha^{\circ}=\bigvee\limits_{k\in K}\bigwedge\limits_{j\in J_{k}}\alpha_{k,j}

Here for
f_{k,j}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot x_{i}+c
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{k,j}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\mathtt{b}^{+}(\phi_{i})\geqslant 1-c&\text{ if }t_{k,j}=f^{\sharp}_{k,j}\\
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\cdot\mathtt{b}^{+}(\phi_{i})<-c&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

Proposition 4.44
.

Let
\Xi\cup\{\alpha\}
be a set of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}
two-layer formulas. Then,
\Xi\!\vDash_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\L}}{\mbox{\rm\scriptsize\L}}{\mbox{\rm\tiny\L}}}^{2}}\!\alpha
iff
\Xi^{\circ}\!\vDash_{\mathtt{b}\mathsf{BD}}\!\alpha^{\circ}
.

5
Conclusion

Let us recapitulate the main results of our paper. We developed the axiomatics of non-standard belief and plausibility functions (Section
3
) and proved their completeness (Theorems
3.12
and
3.13
). We then proposed two different kinds of formalising reasoning with non-standard probabilities and belief functions (Section
4
). In both cases, we show how to reason with inconsistent and incomplete information in a non-trivial manner. We established completeness of these logics: Theorems
4.24
and
4.29
give completeness of two-layered logics while Theorems
4.3
and
4.8
provide completeness of calculi of inequalities
à la
Fagin, Halpern, and Megiddo
[
23
]
. Finally, we defined faithful translations in the manner of
[
3
]
between these two frameworks in Propositions
4.37
,
4.39
,
4.42
, and
4.44
.

Still, several important questions remain unanswered. First, in the case of classical probability theory, belief and plausibility functions are not the only uncertainty measures more general than probabilities proper. In particular, probabilities can be generalised by lower and upper probabilities (cf., e.g.
[
32
, §2.3]
for more details). It would be instructive to define their
\mathsf{BD}
counterparts and investigate their properties. Second, Bayes’ and Jeffrey’s conditionings of non-standard probabilities are given in
[
33
]
. It is reasonable to construct conditioning of non-standard belief and plausibility functions. Furthermore, to the best of our knowledge, there is no work done on the conditioning of non-standard probabilities on arbitrary lattices.

On the logic side, we plan to investigate the proof theory of our two-layered logics. We are particularly interested in providing structural proof theory in the form of display or sequent calculi and in expanding on the tableaux framework presented in
[
8
]
. The tableaux will also allow us to establish the decidability and complexity bounds of our two-layered logics.

Appendix A
Proofs of Section
2.3

The following Lemma is used to prove Theorem
2.9

Lemma A.1
.

[
49
, Lemma 2.6]

Let
\mu:{\mathcal{P}}(X)\to\mathbb{R}
be a function such that:

\mu(\varnothing)=0
and
